float s3110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

```c
float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    float local_max;
    int local_xindex, local_yindex;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        local_max = aa[0][0];
        local_xindex = 0;
        local_yindex = 0;
        
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_yindex = 0;
            
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                    row_yindex = j;
                }
            }
            
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

## FEEDBACK OUTPUT
## Analysis Report  

### 1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code  
The optimized code compiles without errors.  

### 2. Semantic Comparison with Simple Test Cases  
The optimized code introduces a **semantic error**.  

**Reason**:  
In the source code, when `aa[i][j] > max`, both `max` and the corresponding indices `xindex` and `yindex` are updated simultaneously.  
In the optimized code, the inner loop finds the maximum per row (`row_max`) and its column index (`row_yindex`), then compares `row_max` with `local_max` only after the inner loop finishes.  
However, if two rows have the same maximum value, the source code would pick the first occurrence (since it updates only when `aa[i][j] > max`, not `>=`).  
The optimized code picks the last row with that maximum value because it updates `local_max` when `row_max > local_max` (strict inequality), but if `row_max == local_max`, it does not update indices, which matches the source code’s behavior for equal maxima.  

But there’s a subtle bug:  
- In the source code, when `aa[i][j] == max`, indices are **not** updated.  
- In the optimized code, if `row_max == local_max`, indices are **not** updated, which matches.  
- However, when `row_max > local_max`, the optimized code updates `local_yindex` to `row_yindex`, which is correct.  

**But** the optimized code initializes `row_yindex = 0` and starts `j` from 1, so if the maximum in a row is at column 0, `row_yindex` remains 0, which is correct.  

**Test case to reveal the bug**:  
Let `aa[0][1] = 10` (max in row 0), `aa[1][0] = 10` (same max in row 1).  
- Source code: scans row-major, finds `aa[0][1] = 10`, sets `max=10, xindex=0, yindex=1`. Then in row 1, `aa[1][0] = 10` is not greater than `max`, so indices stay (0,1).  
- Optimized code:  
  - Row 0: `row_max=10, row_yindex=1`.  
  - Row 1: `row_max=10, row_yindex=0`.  
  Since `row_max == local_max` (10), indices are not updated, so final indices are (0,1). ✅ Same.  

So the semantics match.  

**But wait**: The compiler feedback says “loop not vectorized: value that could not be identified as reduction is used outside the loop.” This is because `row_yindex` is not a pure reduction variable (it’s not associative), so vectorization fails.  

**Conclusion**: Semantics are correct.  

### 3. Vectorization Analysis  
The compiler feedback indicates the inner loop over `j` is not vectorized because `row_yindex` is not a reduction variable in the classical sense (max value with index tracking is not a simple reduction).  

**Why vectorization fails**:  
- The loop has a conditional update of `row_max` and `row_yindex`.  
- `row_yindex` depends on which element is the max, so it’s not a reduction that can be computed in parallel easily.  

**Potential improvement**:  
To enable vectorization, we can separate the reduction:  
1. Find `row_max` using vectorized max reduction.  
2. Then find the first occurrence of `row_max` in that row to get `row_yindex`.  

But this changes semantics slightly (source code picks first occurrence of max in row-major order; finding first occurrence after max is known still matches).  

However, the current optimized code already fails vectorization due to `row_yindex`.  

**Optimization opportunity exists** (vectorization is possible with restructuring), so we output **FAIL**.  

### 4. Final Decision  
FAIL — because there is an opportunity to restructure the code to enable vectorization.

Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body

     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    float local_max;
    int local_xindex, local_yindex;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        local_max = aa[0][0];
        local_xindex = 0;
        local_yindex = 0;
        
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_yindex = 0;
            
            // First loop: find row_max (pure reduction, vectorizable)
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Second loop: find first occurrence of row_max
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                    row_yindex = j;
                    break;
                }
            }
            
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

## Analysis  

### 1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code

### 2. Semantic Comparison  

The **source code** finds the **maximum value** in a 256×256 array and records the **first occurrence** (lowest `i`, then lowest `j`) where that maximum appears.  
The **optimized code** changes this behavior:  

- It first finds the row maximum (`row_max`) for each row `i`.  
- Then it finds the **first occurrence** of `row_max` in that row.  
- It compares `row_max` with `local_max` across rows.  

**Problem**: If multiple rows have the same maximum value, the source code would pick the first occurrence in **row-major order** (row-by-row, column-by-column).  
The optimized code picks the **first row** where that maximum appears **when scanning rows**, but within that row, it finds the first column where `row_max` occurs. This matches the source code’s behavior **only if** the row maximum is indeed the global maximum for that row.  

But wait — the optimized code’s `row_max` is computed correctly per row, but the **global maximum tracking** is done across rows, so the first row with the global max will be chosen, and within that row, the first column with that max will be chosen. This is **exactly** row-major order first occurrence.  

Let’s test with a simple example:  

**Example 1**  
Array:  
Row 0: [5, 6]  
Row 1: [6, 4]  

Source code:  
- Scans (0,0)=5, (0,1)=6 → max=6, x=0, y=1.  

Optimized code:  
Row 0: row_max=6, first at j=1. local_max=6, x=0, y=1.  
Row 1: row_max=6, first at j=0. But 6 is not > 6, so no update.  
Result: x=0, y=1 ✅  

**Example 2**  
Array:  
Row 0: [7, 5]  
Row 1: [7, 8]  

Source:  
(0,0)=7 → max=7, x=0, y=0.  

Optimized:  
Row 0: row_max=7, first at j=0. local_max=7, x=0, y=0.  
Row 1: row_max=8, first at j=1. 8>7 → update: x=1, y=1.  
Source would have found 8 at (1,1) later anyway, so final max=8, x=1, y=1 in both? Wait, source would find 8 at (1,1) and update max to 8, x=1, y=1. Yes, same. ✅  

**Example 3**  
Array:  
Row 0: [5, 5, 5]  
Row 1: [5, 5, 5]  

Source: max=5, x=0, y=0.  

Optimized:  
Row 0: row_max=5, first at j=0. local_max=5, x=0, y=0.  
Row 1: row_max=5, first at j=0. 5 not >5, so no update. ✅  

So semantics are preserved.  

The unit test passed, confirming semantic equivalence.

### 3. Vectorization Analysis  

Compiler feedback:  
- First inner loop (`for j` finding `row_max`) is **vectorized** (width 4, interleaved 4).  
- Second inner loop (`for j` finding first occurrence of `row_max`) is **not vectorized** because loop iterations depend on a conditional break (cannot determine trip count for vectorization).  

This second loop prevents full vectorization of the row processing.  

**Optimization opportunity**:  
We can avoid the second loop entirely by tracking the column index during the first max-finding loop. But that introduces a dependency (max value and its index must be updated together), which may hinder vectorization of the max reduction.  

However, max reduction with index tracking can be vectorized using SIMD with some post-processing (e.g., compare vector of max values, find index within vector lanes). But automatic vectorization may fail due to the index update being a loop-carried dependency.  

The current optimized code splits the reduction into two loops, losing performance due to:  
1. Extra pass over data (second loop).  
2. Non-vectorized second loop.  

**Better approach**:  
Keep original nested loop but ensure compiler can vectorize by:  
- Using `#pragma omp simd reduction(max:max)` and manual index tracking with local variables.  
Or  
- Finding max per row with index in one loop (still may not auto-vectorize due to index dependency), but better than two loops.  

Given the compiler feedback shows **loop not vectorized** for the second inner loop, there is **optimization opportunity** to improve vectorization.  

---

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    float local_max;
    int local_xindex, local_yindex;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        local_max = aa[0][0];
        local_xindex = 0;
        local_yindex = 0;
        
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_yindex = 0;
            
            // Vectorizable max reduction without branching
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                    row_yindex = j;  // Track index during max reduction
                }
            }
            
            // Compare with global max (no inner loop dependency)
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

The optimized code picks the **last** in the row for `row_yindex` (since `row_yindex = j` updates even if `current == row_max`).

UNIT TEST PASS

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_first_yindex = 0;
            
            // Find maximum value in this row
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                    row_first_yindex = j;
                }
            }
            
            // Now find first occurrence of row_max in this row
            // This ensures we get the first occurrence like original code
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                    row_first_yindex = j;
                    break;
                }
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_first_yindex;
            } else if (row_max == local_max) {
                // Preserve first occurrence semantics
                if (i < local_xindex) {
                    // Earlier row with same max value
                    local_xindex = i;
                    local_yindex = row_first_yindex;
                } else if (i == local_xindex) {
                    // Same row, keep minimum column index
                    if (row_first_yindex < local_yindex) {
                        local_yindex = row_first_yindex;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently - enables row-wise vectorization
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_yindex = 0;
            
            // Vector-friendly: find maximum value in this row
            // No early break, uniform execution path
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                // Use conditional assignment without branching
                int is_greater = current > row_max;
                row_max = is_greater ? current : row_max;
                row_yindex = is_greater ? j : row_yindex;
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum - separate from inner loop
            // This enables vectorization of the inner loop
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            } else if (row_max == local_max) {
                // Handle ties: prefer earlier row, then earlier column
                int earlier_row = i < local_xindex;
                int same_row = i == local_xindex;
                int earlier_col = row_yindex < local_yindex;
                
                if (earlier_row) {
                    local_xindex = i;
                    local_yindex = row_yindex;
                } else if (same_row && earlier_col) {
                    local_yindex = row_yindex;
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently - enables row-wise vectorization
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_yindex = 0;
            
            // Vector-friendly: find maximum value in this row
            // No early break, uniform execution path
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                // Use conditional assignment without branching
                int is_greater = current > row_max;
                row_max = is_greater ? current : row_max;
                row_yindex = is_greater ? j : row_yindex;
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum - separate from inner loop
            // This enables vectorization of the inner loop
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            } else if (row_max == local_max) {
                // Handle ties: prefer earlier row, then earlier column
                int earlier_row = i < local_xindex;
                int same_row = i == local_xindex;
                int earlier_col = row_yindex < local_yindex;
                
                if (earlier_row) {
                    local_xindex = i;
                    local_yindex = row_yindex;
                } else if (same_row && earlier_col) {
                    local_yindex = row_yindex;
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;

                    local_yindex = row_yindex;

            }

        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

float s3110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }

    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_yindex = 0;
            
            // First pass: find maximum value in row (vectorizable)
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Second pass: find first occurrence of maximum (vectorizable for comparison)
            // This separates the index tracking from max finding
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                    row_yindex = j;
                    break;  // Found first occurrence in this row
                }
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            } else if (row_max == local_max) {
                // Handle ties: prefer earlier row, then earlier column
                if (i < local_xindex) {
                    local_xindex = i;
                    local_yindex = row_yindex;
                } else if (i == local_xindex && row_yindex < local_yindex) {
                    local_yindex = row_yindex;
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            
            // First pass: find maximum value in row (vectorizable)
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Second pass: find column index of maximum
            // Remove early break to enable vectorization
            int row_yindex = 0;
            float max_diff = 0.0f;
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                // Use arithmetic to find first occurrence without branching
                float diff = row_max - aa[i][j];
                // diff == 0.0f for the maximum element
                int is_max = (diff == 0.0f);
                // Update only if this is the first maximum found
                row_yindex = (max_diff == 0.0f && is_max) ? j : row_yindex;
                max_diff = (max_diff == 0.0f && is_max) ? 1.0f : max_diff;
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum using arithmetic to avoid branching
            float diff = row_max - local_max;
            int update_global = (diff > 0.0f);
            int equal_max = (diff == 0.0f);
            
            // Update global max if row_max > local_max
            local_max = update_global ? row_max : local_max;
            local_xindex = update_global ? i : local_xindex;
            local_yindex = update_global ? row_yindex : local_yindex;
            
            // Handle ties: prefer earlier row, then earlier column
            int earlier_row = equal_max && (i < local_xindex);
            int same_row_earlier_col = equal_max && (i == local_xindex) && (row_yindex < local_yindex);
            
            local_xindex = earlier_row ? i : local_xindex;
            local_yindex = earlier_row ? row_yindex : local_yindex;
            local_yindex = same_row_earlier_col ? row_yindex : local_yindex;
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

    - `same_row_earlier_col` condition is redundant since `i == local_xindex` can't happen when `equal_max` is true (because `local_xindex` would be from a previous row)

But consider `aa[0][0] = 10.0`, `aa[0][1] = 9.0`, `aa[1][0] = 10.0`:

float s3110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            
            // First pass: find maximum value in row (vectorizable)
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Second pass: find column index of maximum
            // Remove early break to enable vectorization
            int row_yindex = 0;
            float max_diff = 0.0f;
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                // Use arithmetic to find first occurrence without branching
                float diff = row_max - aa[i][j];
                // diff == 0.0f for the maximum element
                int is_max = (diff == 0.0f);
                // Update only if this is the first maximum found
                row_yindex = (max_diff == 0.0f && is_max) ? j : row_yindex;
                max_diff = (max_diff == 0.0f && is_max) ? 1.0f : max_diff;
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum using arithmetic to avoid branching
            float diff = row_max - local_max;
            int update_global = (diff > 0.0f);
            int equal_max = (diff == 0.0f);
            
            // Update global max if row_max > local_max
            local_max = update_global ? row_max : local_max;
            local_xindex = update_global ? i : local_xindex;
            local_yindex = update_global ? row_yindex : local_yindex;
            
            // Handle ties: prefer earlier row, then earlier column
            int earlier_row = equal_max && (i < local_xindex);
            int same_row_earlier_col = equal_max && (i == local_xindex) && (row_yindex < local_yindex);
            
            local_xindex = earlier_row ? i : local_xindex;
            local_yindex = earlier_row ? row_yindex : local_yindex;
            local_yindex = same_row_earlier_col ? row_yindex : local_yindex;
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.

    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

### **2. Semantic comparison with simple test cases**  

The original code finds the **global maximum** in a 256×256 array, and if multiple positions have the same max value, it picks the **last one in row-major order** (because the inner loops go `i=0..255`, `j=0..255`, and `if (aa[i][j] > max)` updates indices only for `>` not `>=`).  

The optimized code changes this behavior:  
- It first finds the max in each row (`row_max`).  
- Then it finds the **first** occurrence of `row_max` in that row (break on `==`).  
- When comparing row maxima to `local_max`, it uses `>` to update, but for ties (`row_max == local_max`), it picks the **earlier** in row-major order (smaller `i`, or same `i` with smaller `j`).  

This is **not** the same as the original semantics.  

**Example to show difference**:  
Suppose `aa[0][1] = 10`, `aa[0][2] = 10`, `aa[1][0] = 10`.  
Original:  
- i=0, j=1: max=10, x=0, y=1  
- i=0, j=2: max=10, x=0, y=2 (since equal, no update)  
- i=1, j=0: max=10, x=1, y=0 (since equal, no update)  
Final: (0,2)  

Optimized:  
- Row 0: row_max=10, first occurrence at j=1.  
- Row 1: row_max=10, first occurrence at j=0.  
- Compare row 0 max (10) with local_max (init aa[0][0] maybe smaller, so set local_max=10, x=0, y=1).  
- Row 1 max =10 == local_max, i=1 > local_xindex=0, so no update.  
Final: (0,1) → different from (0,2).  

Thus **semantics differ**.  

---

Since semantics are incorrect, we stop here.

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            
            // First pass: find maximum value in row (vectorizable)
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Second pass: find first occurrence of row_max in row-major order
            int row_yindex = 0;
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                    row_yindex = j;
                    break;
                }
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            } else if (row_max == local_max) {
                // Handle ties according to row-major order
                if (i < local_xindex) {
                    local_xindex = i;
                    local_yindex = row_yindex;
                } else if (i == local_xindex && row_yindex < local_yindex) {
                    local_yindex = row_yindex;
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

### **2. Semantic comparison with simple test cases**  

The original code finds the **global maximum** in a 256×256 array, and if multiple positions have the same max value, it picks the **last one in row-major order** (because the inner loops go `i=0..255`, `j=0..255`, and `if (aa[i][j] > max)` updates indices only for `>` not `>=`).  

The optimized code changes this behavior:  
- It first finds the max in each row (`row_max`).  
- Then it finds the **first** occurrence of `row_max` in that row (break on `==`).  
- When comparing row maxima to `local_max`, it uses `>` to update, but for ties (`row_max == local_max`), it picks the **earlier** in row-major order (smaller `i`, or same `i` with smaller `j`).  

This is **not** the same as the original semantics.  

**Example to show difference**:  
Suppose `aa[0][1] = 10`, `aa[0][2] = 10`, `aa[1][0] = 10`.  
Original:  
- i=0, j=1: max=10, x=0, y=1  
- i=0, j=2: max=10, x=0, y=2 (since equal, no update)  
- i=1, j=0: max=10, x=1, y=0 (since equal, no update)  
Final: (0,2)

- Row 0: row_max=10, first occurrence at j=1.  
- Row 1: row_max=10, first occurrence at j=0.  
- Compare row 0 max (10) with local_max (init aa[0][0] maybe smaller, so set local_max=10, x=0, y=1).  
- Row 1 max =10 == local_max, i=1 > local_xindex=0, so no update.  
Final: (0,1) → different from (0,2).  

Thus **semantics differ**.

Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

### 1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code

**Problem Identified**: The optimized code has different semantics from the source code.

**Source Code Behavior**:
- Finds the **first occurrence** of the maximum value in row-major order (i.e., when `aa[i][j] > max`, it updates indices immediately).
- If multiple elements have the same maximum value, it keeps the indices of the **first encountered** maximum.

**Optimized Code Behavior**:
- First pass finds the maximum value in each row (vectorizable).
- Second pass finds the **last occurrence** of that maximum value in the row (`row_yindex = j` updates on every match, ending with the last match).
- When comparing rows, if `row_max == local_max`, it updates indices only if the current position is **later** in row-major order.

This means:
- For duplicate maximum values, the optimized code returns the **last occurrence** in row-major order.
- The source code returns the **first occurrence**.

**Example Verification**:
Consider a 2×2 array:
```
aa[0][0] = 1.0, aa[0][1] = 2.0
aa[1][0] = 2.0, aa[1][1] = 1.0
```
- Source code: max=2.0, xindex=0, yindex=1 (first occurrence at [0][1])
- Optimized code: max=2.0, xindex=1, yindex=0 (last occurrence at [1][0])

The final return value differs because `xindex` and `yindex` are different.

**Conclusion**: The optimized code changes the tie-breaking behavior, making it semantically incorrect.

Since the semantics are incorrect, we stop here. The vectorization analysis (which shows partial vectorization) is irrelevant because the code doesn't produce correct results.

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_yindex = 0;
            
            // First pass: find maximum value in row (vectorizable)
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Second pass: find first occurrence of row_max in row-major order
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                    row_yindex = j;
                    break;  // Stop at first occurrence
                }
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum (preserving original tie-breaking)
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

If `current == row_max`, keep earlier j (since we want first occurrence).  
This eliminates the second loop entirely, making the whole row processing vectorizable for max+index.

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

## **2. Semantic comparison**

Source code logic:  
- Finds the **global maximum** in `aa[256][256]`.
- If multiple elements have the same max value, the **last occurrence** (in row-major order) is recorded because the check is `if (aa[i][j] > max)` (strict greater), so equal values don’t update indices.

Optimized code logic:  
- For each row, finds the **first occurrence** of the row’s maximum (since row scan starts from `j=1` and only updates if `current > row_max`).
- Then compares row_max with local_max using `>`: if equal, no update.  
  So for equal maxima across rows, the first row (in scanning order) containing that max value wins, and within that row, the first column containing that max wins.

But in the original code, scanning all `(i,j)` in order:  
If `aa[i][j] == current_max`, no update. So the first occurrence in the entire scan is kept.  
In the optimized version, the first occurrence **within each row** is found, but when comparing rows, if two rows have the same max value, the first row (smaller i) wins — same as original.

i=1,j=0: 9>8 → max=9, x=1,y=0  
i=1,j=1: 7 not >9 → no change  
Result: max=9, x=1, y=0.

Row1: max=9, yindex=0

Row1: max=10, yindex=0

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body

       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A

     }

     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Second pass: find first index where value equals row_max
            // This separates the argmax from the max reduction
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                    row_yindex = j;
                    break;
                }
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

float s3110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_yindex = 0;
            
            // First pass: find max value in row (pure reduction, vectorizable)
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Second pass: find first index where value equals row_max
            // This separates the argmax from the max reduction
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                    row_yindex = j;
                    break;
                }
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            
            // First pass: find max value in row (pure reduction, vectorizable)
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum first (reorder to avoid unnecessary work)
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                
                // Second pass: find first index where value equals row_max
                // Only executed when this row has the new global maximum
                int row_yindex = 0;
                for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                    if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                        row_yindex = j;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
            // Handle the case where row_max equals local_max but row index is smaller
            else if (row_max == local_max && i < local_xindex) {
                local_xindex = i;
                
                // Find first occurrence in this row
                int row_yindex = 0;
                for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                    if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                        row_yindex = j;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

Optimize code:
```c

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Process each row independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            
            // First pass: find max value in row (pure reduction, vectorizable)
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            
            // Compare with global maximum first (reorder to avoid unnecessary work)
            if (row_max > local_max) {
                local_max = row_max;
                local_xindex = i;
                
                // Second pass: find first index where value equals row_max
                // Only executed when this row has the new global maximum
                int row_yindex = 0;
                for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                    if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                        row_yindex = j;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
            // Handle the case where row_max equals local_max but row index is smaller
            else if (row_max == local_max && i < local_xindex) {
                local_xindex = i;
                
                // Find first occurrence in this row
                int row_yindex = 0;
                for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                    if (aa[i][j] == row_max) {
                        row_yindex = j;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                local_yindex = row_yindex;
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

### 2. Semantic Comparison
**FAIL - Semantic differences detected**

The optimized code has a critical semantic difference from the source code:

**Source Code Behavior:**
- Finds the **first occurrence** of the maximum value when scanning row-major order (i → j)
- For equal maximum values, selects the one with smallest (i,j) in row-major order

**Optimized Code Behavior:**
- First finds row maximums, then compares them
- When a row has a new global maximum, it finds the **first occurrence** within that row
- However, when `row_max == local_max && i < local_xindex`, it only updates if the row index is smaller

**Example demonstrating the issue:**

Consider this 2x2 array:
```
aa[0][0] = 10.0
aa[0][1] = 10.0  ← first maximum in row-major order
aa[1][0] = 10.0
aa[1][1] = 10.0
```

**Source Code Result:**
- Scans (0,0): max=10.0, xindex=0, yindex=0
- Scans (0,1): max=10.0, xindex=0, yindex=0 (no change, first occurrence kept)
- Returns: xindex=0, yindex=0

**Optimized Code Result:**
- Row 0: row_max=10.0, sets local_max=10.0, local_xindex=0, finds first occurrence at j=0
- Row 1: row_max=10.0, equal to local_max, i=1 > local_xindex=0, so doesn't update
- Returns: xindex=0, yindex=0 (correct in this case, but not guaranteed)

**More problematic example:**
```
aa[0][0] = 5.0
aa[0][1] = 10.0  ← should be selected
aa[1][0] = 10.0
aa[1][1] = 10.0
```

- Scans (0,0): max=5.0, xindex=0, yindex=0
- Scans (0,1): max=10.0, xindex=0, yindex=1
- Scans (1,0): max=10.0, xindex=0, yindex=1 (no change)
- Returns: xindex=0, yindex=1

**Optimized Code:**
- Row 0: row_max=10.0, sets local_max=10.0, local_xindex=0, finds first occurrence at j=1
- Row 1: row_max=10.0, equal to local_max, i=1 > local_xindex=0, doesn't update
- Returns: xindex=0, yindex=1 (correct, but due to different logic)

**The fundamental issue:** The optimized code's logic for handling equal maximum values is incomplete and doesn't guarantee the same selection criteria as the source code.

- The two loops searching for the first occurrence of the maximum value (lines 36 and 50) are not vectorized because they contain early exits (break statements)

**Vectorization Limitations:**

2. The algorithm structure creates loop-carried dependencies through `local_max`, `local_xindex`, and `local_yindex`

The current implementation has several inefficiencies:
