2. **Non-vectorizable loops:** The index search loops break vectorization

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];

        int local_yindex = 0;

        float row_maxs[256];
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            row_maxs[i] = row_max;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find global maximum across rows
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_maxs[i] > local_max) {
                local_max = row_maxs[i];
                local_xindex = i;
            }

        // Only need to check rows that could contain the maximum
        int found = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i <= local_xindex && !found; i++) {
            if (row_maxs[i] == local_max) {

                    if (aa[i][j] == local_max) {
                        local_xindex = i;
                        local_yindex = j;
                        found = 1;
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

The original code:

- Finds the **first occurrence** of the maximum value (since `if (aa[i][j] > max)` updates indices only when strictly greater).  
- Returns `max + xindex+1 + yindex+1`.  

The optimized code:  
- First pass: finds row maximums (vectorizable).  
- Second pass: finds which row has the global maximum (but only records the last such row if multiple rows have the same max).  
- Third pass: scans rows from the start until finding the first row with the global max, then scans that row for the first occurrence of that value.  

This matches the original’s “first occurrence” behavior because:  
1. The second pass picks the **last** row with the global max (since it updates `local_xindex` whenever `row_maxs[i] > local_max` or `row_maxs[i] == local_max`? Wait — actually, it only updates on `>` in the second pass, so if two rows have the same max, it picks the first one encountered? Let’s check carefully.)  

Actually, in the second pass:  
```c
if (row_maxs[i] > local_max) {
    local_max = row_maxs[i];
    local_xindex = i;
}
```
If two rows have the same max value, the second one won’t update `local_max` or `local_xindex` because it’s not greater. So `local_xindex` will be the **first** row with that max. Good.  

Then the third pass:  
It loops `i` from 0 to `local_xindex` and stops at the first row where `row_maxs[i] == local_max`. That’s the same first row with the max. Then it scans that row for the first column with that max.  

So the indices match the original.  

But there’s a subtlety: The original scans rows 0..255, columns 0..255, and updates indices only when `aa[i][j] > max`.  
The optimized code’s third pass scans columns 0..255 in that first max row until it finds `aa[i][j] == local_max`.  
Since the original would have updated `xindex, yindex` at the first occurrence of the max in that row, that’s exactly what the optimized code does.  

So semantics appear correct.

Source code: 
```c
float s3110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // First pass: find row maximums (vectorizable reduction)
        float row_maxs[256];
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                }
            }
            row_maxs[i] = row_max;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find global maximum across rows
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_maxs[i] > local_max) {
                local_max = row_maxs[i];
                local_xindex = i;
            }
        }
        
        // Third pass: find first occurrence of global maximum
        // Only need to check rows that could contain the maximum
        int found = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i <= local_xindex && !found; i++) {
            if (row_maxs[i] == local_max) {
                // Scan this row for the first occurrence
                for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                    if (aa[i][j] == local_max) {
                        local_xindex = i;
                        local_yindex = j;
                        found = 1;
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

```c
float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // First pass: find row maximums (fully vectorizable)
        float row_maxs[256];
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            // Vectorizable reduction without branching
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                row_max = (current > row_max) ? current : row_max;
            }
            row_maxs[i] = row_max;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find first row with global maximum (vectorizable)
        int max_rows[256];
        int max_row_count = 0;
        float global_max = row_maxs[0];
        
        // Find global max value first
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            global_max = (row_maxs[i] > global_max) ? row_maxs[i] : global_max;
        }
        
        // Collect all rows with global max
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_maxs[i] == global_max) {
                max_rows[max_row_count++] = i;
            }
        }
        
        // Third pass: find first occurrence in first qualifying row
        local_max = global_max;
        local_xindex = max_rows[0];
        
        // Vectorizable search for first occurrence in this row
        int found_col = -1;
        float* row_ptr = aa[local_xindex];
        for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
            if (row_ptr[j] == global_max) {
                found_col = j;
                break;
            }
        }
        local_yindex = found_col;
        
        // Handle case where we need to check earlier rows with same max
        for (int r = 0; r < max_row_count; r++) {
            int test_row = max_rows[r];
            if (test_row > local_xindex) break;
            
            row_ptr = aa[test_row];
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (row_ptr[j] == global_max) {
                    local_xindex = test_row;
                    local_yindex = j;
                    r = max_row_count; // exit outer loop
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

Source code: 
```c
float s3110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // First pass: find row maximums (fully vectorizable)
        float row_maxs[256];
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            // Vectorizable reduction without branching
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                row_max = (current > row_max) ? current : row_max;
            }
            row_maxs[i] = row_max;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find first row with global maximum (vectorizable)
        int max_rows[256];
        int max_row_count = 0;
        float global_max = row_maxs[0];
        
        // Find global max value first
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            global_max = (row_maxs[i] > global_max) ? row_maxs[i] : global_max;
        }
        
        // Collect all rows with global max
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_maxs[i] == global_max) {
                max_rows[max_row_count++] = i;
            }
        }
        
        // Third pass: find first occurrence in first qualifying row
        local_max = global_max;
        local_xindex = max_rows[0];
        
        // Vectorizable search for first occurrence in this row
        int found_col = -1;
        float* row_ptr = aa[local_xindex];
        for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
            if (row_ptr[j] == global_max) {
                found_col = j;
                break;
            }
        }
        local_yindex = found_col;
        
        // Handle case where we need to check earlier rows with same max
        for (int r = 0; r < max_row_count; r++) {
            int test_row = max_rows[r];
            if (test_row > local_xindex) break;
            
            row_ptr = aa[test_row];
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (row_ptr[j] == global_max) {
                    local_xindex = test_row;
                    local_yindex = j;
                    r = max_row_count; // exit outer loop
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = local_max;
        xindex = local_xindex;
        yindex = local_yindex;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

UNIT TEST PASS

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

Source code: 
```c
float s3110(int iterations,float aa[256][256])
{
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        max = aa[(0)][0];
        xindex = 0;
        yindex = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (aa[i][j] > max) {
                    max = aa[i][j];
                    xindex = i;
                    yindex = j;
                }
            }
        }
        chksum = max + (float) xindex + (float) yindex;
    }
    return max + xindex+1 + yindex+1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // First pass: find row maximums (fully vectorizable)
        float row_maxs[256];
        int row_max_cols[256];
        
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_max_col = 0;
            
            // Vectorizable reduction without branching
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                if (current > row_max) {
                    row_max = current;
                    row_max_col = j;
                }
            }
            row_maxs[i] = row_max;
            row_max_cols[i] = row_max_col;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find global maximum and its first occurrence
        // Split into two independent loops for better vectorization
        float global_max = row_maxs[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            global_max = (row_maxs[i] > global_max) ? row_maxs[i] : global_max;
        }
        
        // Find first row with global maximum
        int first_max_row = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (row_maxs[i] == global_max) {
                first_max_row = i;
                break;
            }
        }
        
        // Find first column with global_max in first_max_row
        // Use temporary pointer for better vectorization
        float* row_ptr = aa[first_max_row];
        int first_max_col = row_max_cols[first_max_row];
        
        // Verify this is actually the first occurrence in row-major order
        // by checking if any earlier row has the same max at earlier column
        for (int i = 0; i < first_max_row; i++) {
            if (row_maxs[i] == global_max) {
                // Check if this row has the max at an earlier column
                if (row_max_cols[i] < first_max_col) {
                    first_max_row = i;
                    first_max_col = row_max_cols[i];
                }
            }
        }
        
        max = global_max;
        xindex = first_max_row;
        yindex = first_max_col;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

```

     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // First pass: find row maximums (fully vectorizable)
        float row_maxs[256];
        int row_max_cols[256];
        
        // Split each row reduction into two phases for better vectorization
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            // Phase 1: Find maximum value in row (vectorizable)
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                // Use conditional move pattern (compiler can vectorize with max/min)
                row_max = (current > row_max) ? current : row_max;
            }
            row_maxs[i] = row_max;
            
            // Phase 2: Find first occurrence of maximum in row
            // This can be vectorized with masked operations
            int first_col = 0;
            // Use temporary variable to avoid complex branching
            float* row_ptr = aa[i];
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                // Check if this element equals the maximum and we haven't found one yet
                if (row_ptr[j] == row_max && first_col == 0) {
                    first_col = j + 1;  // Store as j+1 to distinguish from 0
                }
            }
            // Convert back to 0-based index
            row_max_cols[i] = (first_col > 0) ? first_col - 1 : 0;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find global maximum from row maximums (vectorizable)
        float global_max = row_maxs[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            global_max = (row_maxs[i] > global_max) ? row_maxs[i] : global_max;
        }
        
        // Find first occurrence in row-major order
        // Use a single pass without early exit for better vectorization
        int found_row = -1;
        int found_col = -1;
        int found = 0;  // Flag to indicate if found
        
        // Process in row-major order
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (!found && row_maxs[i] == global_max) {
                // This row contains the global maximum
                // Check columns in this row
                float* row_ptr = aa[i];
                for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                    if (!found && row_ptr[j] == global_max) {
                        found_row = i;
                        found_col = j;
                        found = 1;
                        // Continue to maintain uniform loop structure
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        
        // Set results
        max = global_max;
        xindex = found_row;
        yindex = found_col;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

- Returns indices (0,0)

However, there's another issue: The source code uses `>` (strictly greater than) while the optimized code uses `==` for equality checks. This could cause problems with floating-point comparisons where values might be equal within floating-point precision but not exactly equal.

   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];

   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Phase 1: Find row maximums (fully vectorizable)
        float row_maxs[256];
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                row_max = (current > row_max) ? current : row_max;
            }
            row_maxs[i] = row_max;
        }
        
        // Phase 2: Find global maximum from row maximums (vectorizable)
        float global_max = row_maxs[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            global_max = (row_maxs[i] > global_max) ? row_maxs[i] : global_max;
        }
        
        // Phase 3: Find first occurrence in row-major order
        // Use temporary variables to avoid complex branching
        int found_row = 0;
        int found_col = 0;
        int found = 0;
        
        // Process rows in order
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            if (!found && row_maxs[i] == global_max) {
                // This row contains the global maximum
                // Process columns in order without early exit
                float* row_ptr = aa[i];
                for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                    // Use arithmetic to avoid branching
                    int match = (!found && row_ptr[j] == global_max);
                    found_row = match ? i : found_row;
                    found_col = match ? j : found_col;
                    found = match ? 1 : found;
                }
            }
        }
        
        // Set results
        max = global_max;
        xindex = found_row;
        yindex = found_col;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

```c
float s3110_opt(int iterations, float aa[256][256]) {
    int xindex, yindex;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 100*(iterations/(256)); nl++) {
        float local_max = aa[0][0];
        int local_xindex = 0;
        int local_yindex = 0;
        
        // Phase 1: Find row maximums and their column indices (fully vectorizable)
        float row_maxs[256];
        int row_max_cols[256];
        
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            float row_max = aa[i][0];
            int row_col = 0;
            
            for (int j = 1; j < 256; j++) {
                float current = aa[i][j];
                int is_greater = (current > row_max);
                row_max = is_greater ? current : row_max;
                row_col = is_greater ? j : row_col;
            }
            
            row_maxs[i] = row_max;
            row_max_cols[i] = row_col;
        }
        
        // Phase 2: Find global maximum and first occurrence (vectorizable)
        float global_max = row_maxs[0];
        int best_row = 0;
        int best_col = row_max_cols[0];
        
        for (int i = 1; i < 256; i++) {
            float current_max = row_maxs[i];
            int is_greater = (current_max > global_max);
            int is_equal = (current_max == global_max);
            
            // Update if greater, or if equal but earlier in row-major order
            int should_update = is_greater || (is_equal && i < best_row);
            
            global_max = should_update ? current_max : global_max;
            best_row = should_update ? i : best_row;
            best_col = should_update ? row_max_cols[i] : best_col;
        }
        
        // Set results
        max = global_max;
        xindex = best_row;
        yindex = best_col;
        chksum = max + (float)xindex + (float)yindex;
    }
    
    return max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1;
}

## Analysis  

### 1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code  
The optimized code compiles without errors (UNIT TEST PASS indicates this).  
We can proceed to semantic comparison.

---

### 2. Semantic Comparison with Source Code  

**Source code behavior:**  
- Finds the **first occurrence** of the maximum value in row-major order (i.e., if two elements have the same max value, the one with smaller `i` wins; if same `i`, smaller `j` wins).  
- Returns `max + xindex + 1 + yindex + 1` where `xindex`, `yindex` are the indices of the **first occurrence** of the max.

**Optimized code behavior:**  
- Phase 1: For each row, finds the **first occurrence** of the row’s maximum.  
- Phase 2: Compares row maxima to find the global max.  
  - If `current_max > global_max`: update.  
  - If `current_max == global_max`: update only if `i < best_row` (i.e., earlier row).  

This matches the **first occurrence in row-major order** because:  
  1. Row phase picks first max in each row (smallest `j` for that row).  
  2. Global phase picks smallest `i` for equal maxima.  

Thus, semantics are **correct**.  
Unit test passes confirm this.

---

### 3. Vectorization Analysis  

Compiler feedback shows two loops marked as “not vectorized”:  

1. **Inner row loop** (`for j`):  
   - Not vectorized because `row_max` and `row_col` are updated conditionally based on comparisons, and the compiler cannot identify them as a max-reduction with index tracking in a vector-friendly way.  
   - The conditional selection (`is_greater ? current : row_max`) creates a loop-carried dependency that is not a simple reduction pattern recognized by the compiler.

2. **Global max loop** (`for i`):  
   - Similar issue: `global_max`, `best_row`, `best_col` updated conditionally with comparisons involving both value and index tie-breaking.  
   - Compiler cannot see this as a standard reduction.

**Why vectorization fails:**  
- The compiler’s reduction detection requires a single accumulator updated with a commutative/associative operation (like `+`, `max`, `min`).  
- Here, we have **three variables** updated together (`max`, `xindex`, `yindex`) with a tie-breaking rule that depends on original indices, making it a **non-associative** reduction (order matters for ties).  
- This is a **search with early-index preference**, not a pure `max` reduction.

---

### 4. Optimization Opportunities  

The current optimized code **does not vectorize** and is therefore **not fully optimized**.  
We must output **FAIL** because:  
- The compiler feedback shows missed vectorization.  
- There is room for improvement to make loops vectorizable.

**Potential improvements:**  
- For the row max phase: use vectorized `max` operations to find row maxima **values**, then in a separate pass find the first index of that max in each row (less frequent).  
- For global max: find max value vectorially, then find first occurrence in original data in a single pass.  
- Could restructure to separate **value reduction** (vectorizable) from **index selection** (non-vectorizable but cheap).

Since the current code is not vectorized and improvements are possible, the task is not complete.
